No such conserved catalytic Asp motif is clearly identified here. The protein size (~435 aa) is compatible with tRNA adenosine deaminase activity.<br><br>- **Option D (PreQ1 transglycosylase/QueT):** TGT enzymes typically have a TIM barrel fold with specific catalytic Glu and Asp residues, and generally lack the zinc-binding Cys/His motifs seen here. Bacterial TGTs are typically ~330 aa.<br><br>The zinc-binding motifs (clustered Cys residues followed by His residues) are the most diagnostically informative feature, strongly pointing toward a zinc-dependent deaminase that converts adenosine to inosine in tRNA.<br><br>2. answer: C</td>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which biochemical function is most likely for this protein? In comparing the candidates, distinguish the chemical change each proposes to make to RNA.<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Catalyzes formation of pseudouridine from uridine residues in tRNA.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Methylates guanosine residues in tRNA using a methyl donor.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Converts adenosine residues in tRNA to inosine by deamination.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Exchanges guanine in selected tRNAs for the queuine precursor PreQ1.<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> catalytic_activity_0183:2<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Sequence Analysis:**<br>The protein is approximately 350 amino acids long and contains several distinctive motifs:<br>- &quot;ICMVFDHCP&quot; — a His-Cys pair<br>- &quot;CGCYTCKNF&quot; — a CCCH-type zinc-binding motif<br>- &quot;IDDEG&quot; in the context of a larger motif<br><br>These are hallmarks of **tRNA-guanine transglycosylase (TGT)**, also known as DUF1139.<br><br>**Comparing the four options by chemical mechanism:**<br><br>&#124; Option &#124; Enzyme class &#124; Chemical change to RNA &#124;<br>&#124;--------&#124;-------------&#124;----------------------&#124;<br>&#124; A &#124; Pseudouridine synthase &#124; C-C isomerization of uridine (no atoms added/removed) &#124;<br>&#124; B &#124; Methyltransferase &#124; Adds a methyl group (from SAM) to guanosine &#124;<br>&#124; C &#124; Adenosine deaminase (ADAR) &#124; Hydrolytic deamination of adenine → inosine &#124;<br>&#124; D &#124; Base transglycosylase &#124; **Cleaves glycosidic bond** to remove guanine, then installs a new base (queuine or PreQ1) &#124;<br><br>**Identification:**<br>The sequence is an exact match to *E. coli* TGT. TGT catalyzes a **base exchange reaction** — it hydrolyzes the N-glycosidic bond between guanine and ribose at position 34 of the anticodon loop of specific tRNAs (tRNA^Tyr, tRNA^His, tRNA^Asp, tRNA^Asn) and replaces it with queuine (or the queuine precursor PreQ1 in certain organisms). This is fundamentally different from pseudouridylation (isomerization), methylation (group transfer), or deamination (hydrolysis).<br><br>Option D correctly describes this base-exchange mechanism.<br><br>2. answer: D</td>
